## APPROVED AND LOCKED RESEARCH SCOPE | 7 November 2026

**AUTHORITATIVE scope. Explicitly approved and refined by user on 7 November 2026. This section OVERRIDES all older draft interpretations in the historical handover, including old N1/N2/N3 nomenclature and any statement suggesting Study A is already completed.** Only explicit later user corrections can change this contract. It is distinct from the physical modelling assumptions that still need selection. Local export: `case1/RESEARCH_SCOPE_APPROVED_2026-11-07.md` in downloadable `SEEDExchange_ProjectScope_And_AnnualExport_v1.zip` (not yet pushed to GitLab, per local-first rule).

### The question

How can 5–15-house communities in San Rafael, Colombia be supplied economically and electrically with shared PV, battery energy/power and inverter capacity, considering existing EPM LV networks, purpose-designed clustered LV networks and a site-specific shared central installation? Determine self-sufficiency, cost, grid import, transformer/line loading, voltage, losses, AC-constrained active and reactive dispatch, siting, sensitivity and possible benefits of several multi-home shared clusters. The final answers must be compared on controlled household/demand/resource/equipment assumptions, with individually documented source uncertainty. Keep existing historical energy-economic and electrical research intact.

### Study A: existing EPM transformer/LV territory study. FIRST, NOT COMPLETE

Use the **already mapped** EPM asset geometry and explicitly **provisional** household service attachments. The existing `scenario_membership.py` defines (a) a **7-house** cohort on transformer **94022**, (b) a **9-house** cohort on the SAME **94022**, and (c) a **12-house** cohort on transformer **106039**. The broader 94022 GIS territory has **10** provisional houses in the earlier 40-hour study; this is NOT the same profile/customer cohort as the 7- or 9-house cases. Do not call these three separate transformers, and do not assume every 5–15-house economic scenario has an actual mapped EPM LV group. Other mapped territories can be studied where evidence and matching profiles justify it. **Transformer 106039 is modelled as 5 kVA** by explicit user choice; 94022 has archived 5 kVA evidence. Actual asset/nameplate/service-phase precision remains evidence-dependent.

A is the detailed **existing-grid** engineering study. For each physically and source-aligned cohort, compare archive-selected PV panel, BESS module/power and inverter equipment (initial source-defined hardware anchors, expand into non-dominated/transition pool if electrical boundaries require). First reproduce/retain original manufacturer-SOC-constrained 8,760h energy dispatch, then use **existing pandapower GIS and Q-control** to check min/max house voltage, voltage-violation hour counts, line and transformer loading and overload duration, power-flow convergence, losses, grid import/export, zero ENS, self-sufficiency and costs. Perform **line/service-length x1.0/x1.3/x1.6** sensitivities (the exact physical service routes are provisional, preserve true GIS section lengths as independent source evidence), load-PF sensitivity 0.95/0.98/1.00, optional 0.90 stress, reactive Q-capability ratios, voltage-control policies, active-power redispatch, and credible inverter-placement alternatives, with manufacturer minimum physical SOC 20%, real battery/inverter limits, and full-year chronology. Assess AC-constrained control and sizing only where the previously optimal economic equipment fails or near uncertainty margins. **DO NOT rerun the 6,840 investment tasks** or declare absent 8,760h output equivalent to an electrical failure.

The **old 40-critical-hour** head/middle/end/Q-placement work is actual saved evidence, not an annual result. The user is currently finishing **two 12-house equipment annual validations** (54 panels/9 battery modules/20 kVA and 56/10/20; one reported finished, the other planned at time of confirmation). Other saved 7- and 12-house annual passes are conditional models, not entire Study A completion. Only conduct selected annual inverter-placement matched-comparison if it answers a meaningful remaining question **after the primary A cohort/equipment AC validation**, not as the automatic next task.

### Study B: proposed physically coherent 5–15-household community network. SECOND

Use the **existing 35 georeferenced mapped candidate houses**, select spatially compact groups of N=5 to 15 houses and potentially multiple coherent groupings for each N; preserve actual geographical identity and latitude/longitude, document membership per scenario, and keep matched memberships fixed within each controlled comparison. **Do not impose one arbitrary fixed 15-house list for all N**; source-backed spatial clustering and growth scenarios may select different adjacent sets when transparently declared.

**Load diversity:** Each house must have an independent **existing 2025 synthetic stochastic hourly household profile**, not a copy/paste aggregate or one identical trace for all loads. Map and preserve distinct source profile indices and random seeds to house IDs; if new synthetic diversity experiments are needed, retain original baseline and label variations separately with comparable total energy. The `8_homes_H01/H02` economic variants are synthetic profile variants, NOT the mapped physical H01/H02 properties.

Choose a **proposed transformer placement near the group based on an electrically and physically meaningful optimization**, not automatically the geometric centroid. Evaluate transformer kVA adequacy, site/access, construction route length and costs, min voltage and peak loading. **5 kVA is initial hypothetical planning rating**, not guaranteed to supply N=15. Compare **STAR** (individually branched connections from common node) versus **branched MST** (minimum spanning tree, with shared portions carrying downstream demand) topologies. Both are radial in graph theory; the distinction is star versus a minimum-length *branched radial tree*, not radial versus MST as contradictory categories. Straight-line MST is a schematic candidate, not a surveyed viable route or proof of protection/thermal adequacy. Use documented conductor R/X/ampacity/phase/neutral assumptions and explicit uncertainties.

Apply **the same type of analysis used in A** to B once geometry is defined: energy-economic source equipment portfolio, manufacturer-SOC dispatch, baseline power flow, Q ratio and control, inverter placement where informative, active redispatch, annual AC, x1.0/x1.3/x1.6 line length, PF scenarios, voltage and thermal violations, line/trafo loading and network losses, lifetime economic updates. REUSE the existing EPM/pandapower/PyPSA engine components; only new physical geometry/grouping and connection definitions should be developed.

### Study C: Site A shared PV+BESS clearing and connection. THIRD

Use Site A `(6.248376671296207, -75.00932156861505)`, current user-digitized ~1759m² *gross* clearing polygon and existing NASA/pvlib solar/obstruction sensitivity. First investigate **PV+BESS physically at Site A connected by a new proposed line to existing 94022 LV**, optionally using the closest mapped GIS section `G3E_FID=47053144` as a *candidate* endpoint. The ~192m site-to-section map distance is **geometric**, not routed cable length and not evidence that a connection currently exists. Test inverter P/Q, PF, cable current, transformer kVA and spare headroom, voltage min/max, power-flow losses, control, energy dispatch, connection/approval and total costs, and exact nearby-house service mapping. No invented existing wire.

**If C's 94022 option is electrically or economically unsuitable**, assess a **new transformer at Site A** with physically proposed star or MST feeders to appropriately nearby homes. The user's earlier explicit baseline is a **new 5 kVA transformer**, with possible 10/15 kVA sensitivity if actually needed. Their latest spoken 'new 5–15 transformer' phrase is ambiguous whether referring to a 5–15 kVA rating range; do not silently assume such ratings are approved without discussion. Include transformer upstream electrical connection, costs, protection, phase and physical routing. Do not assume a 5 kVA nameplate is sufficient at 15 homes.

**EXCLUDE THE NORTHERN 25 kVA TRANSFORMER AND ALL ITS CONNECTION PROPOSALS** unconditionally from A/B/C. It was explicitly ruled out by the user even though it appears in old GIS records. Prior mentions are provenance/history only.

### Cross-study architecture: central vs multiple shared multi-home clusters

This is an **additional dimension applied to A/B/C when those network results are usable**, not a separate model that invalidates existing work. Compare (1) a single central shared PV+BESS serving the study households, (2) two or more **shared multi-home clusters** with PV/BESS units located at defensible connection nodes, and optionally (3) hybrid central-plus-satellite shared installations. One system per individual home is a **secondary benchmark**, not the primary scenario. **A's existing 7/9/10/12-house transformer-based geographical groups are useful cluster evidence**, but do not treat 7 and 9 on 94022 as independent utility transformers.

Two distinct comparisons: first **matched total hardware and the same customer/time series**, keeping aggregate PV, battery energy, battery power and inverter AC capability comparable to isolate placement and clustering; second **architecture-specific feasible hardware** to see if savings in network losses/feeder sizes/line headroom justify extra inverters, batteries, control, protection, meters and cables. Second step does NOT require re-solving 6,840 tasks; use archived candidates and targeted optimization only for genuinely new constraints/new separate cluster investments. Investigate potential **inter-cluster benefits**, with strict separation of independent LV territories, commercial energy sharing versus real physical interconnection. Never casually parallel transformer secondaries, pretend cluster transformers have spare headroom or double-count households; a physical tie requires documented feeder/protection/metering and EPM authorization. Existing `case1/studies/annual_shared_pvbess` and `architecture_comparison` prototypes are reusable source work.

### Shared engineering parameters (APPROVED as planning screens, not regulatory certification)

- 2025 gridded NASA weather and **distinct** stochastic annual household profiles, reproducible seeds and profile IDs; not individually metered actual household demand.
- Model **106039=5 kVA**. Load PF 0.98 reference, principal PF 0.95/0.98/1.00, PF 0.90 optional inductive stress only; user report on Colombian PF1 convention is a modelling rationale, **not verified universal grid code or actual household PF measurement**.
- Proposed engineering voltage band **0.95–1.05 pu**, prior **0.97–1.03 pu** as strict sensitivity; transformer and cable <=100% model thermal limits (with uncertainty), separate from local statutory duration rules.
- Manufacturer battery DoD assumption corresponds to **20% minimum physical SOC**; full chronological battery charge/discharge and inverter power constraints. Night reactive Q **off** until manufacturer supports verified auxiliary VAR service.
- PV/BESS options and control, actual LV phases/protection, selected conductor parameters, tariff, site costs and customer-service routing remain source/assumption sensitive; historic 25-year/6%-discount HOMER/quote lifecycle basis is conditional, explicitly price new cables, meters, protection, switching, civil, utility transformer and connection where plausible. Unknown costs are **unknown**, never zero.
- Do not reintroduce invented critical/deferrable-load fractions. Any resilience/islanding analysis is separate from ordinary grid-connected self-sufficiency and requires credible inverter/protection assumptions.

### Required order and safeguards

**A first:** systematically complete equipment selection vs EPM-backed customer-group voltage, loading, line-length, control, PF and SOC-consistent annual feasibility; consolidate user's finished annual candidate jobs and historical source. Existing Q/inverter-location critical-hour studies are inputs.

**B second:** proximity-based 5–15-house spatial grouping, transformer siting, star and MST, independent stochastic house profiles and A's electrical/energy/control workflow, extending stored hardware pool as necessary.

**C third:** Site A-to-94022, then new 5 kVA transformer star/MST fallback and alternate rating sensitivity if needed, with no northern transformer.

**Architecture:** compare central versus two-plus shared clusters and possible interconnection on physically defensible A/B/C cases with matched-hardware controls then separate feasible-equipment cost improvement.

**Code/output discipline:** keep all work under project root, separate versioned scenario inputs/results/figures/manifests, immutable historical output; use available original PyPSA/pandapower/GIS/solar/AC-aware solvers and results. Assist via downloadable LOCAL code/tests then user's local execution/feedback. Synchronize any new code or data **directly to main only after explicit user permission**. Only this rolling handover may be updated on GitLab on each assistant reply with [ci skip]. Don't add tools or fixed 50-run ceilings.

**Implementation-specific choices still open:** B grouping algorithm and candidate groups per N, transformer siting cost/feasibility rules, line routes/conductors, actual service phase/protection and connection permissions, exact technology-to-architecture assignment, Site A tie-cable routing, additional 10/15 kVA rating conditions, and real physical vs accounting-only inter-cluster linkage. Each must be resolved before it is encoded, but they do NOT reopen the approved overall A/B/C study purpose.

Read this file first, then check the live GitLab HEAD and relevant output manifests. **After every substantive reply**, revise this SAME file with decisions, completed actions, results, failures, uncertainties, and next steps. Commit documentation changes with [ci skip] and retain an exportable Markdown copy. Do not recreate a new handover filename. Mark reported, source-verified, planned, running and unverified results distinctly. Preserve all historical numerical results and test evidence. Do not change new topology, equipment ratings, cost assumptions, models or tariffs without discussing substantive design choices with the user. Do not start a new historical optimization sweep simply to reproduce saved outputs.

## Standing research-decision and coding protocol (reconfirmed 6 November 2026)

**This takes precedence over an unqualified "proceed" when a modelling choice remains open.**

1. **Research/audit before coding.** Inspect existing results, source data, GIS geometry, repository history and tests. Use read-only comparisons for unresolved choices.
2. **Discuss substantive choices with the user before implementation.** Present alternatives, physical assumptions, evidence level, advantages/tradeoffs and a recommended choice. Explicitly settle equipment ratings, topology, nested membership, new transformer placement/rating, operating control, PF/voltage limits, costs, tariff, site or protection choices **before encoding them**. Do not infer approval from broad permission to continue.
3. **Once the engineering design is agreed**, implement and test without needless requests for approval of ordinary programming details. Clear bug fixes preserving agreed assumptions can follow a test-first process.

User returns logs/results and iterations remain local. Once changes are substantial and validated, **only after explicit user authorization** synchronize them directly to the existing GitLab **main** branch. Do not create feature branches or merge requests as the default workflow. Never modify code, models, results, site data, or other GitLab files without the user's specific authorization. Documentation-only updates to this handover on main after every reply are the standing exception.
5. Distinguish **approved/implemented/passed/conditional/proposed/open** decisions in all reports. Never invent EPM poles, customer service topology, transformer nameplates, physical connection routes, phase systems, or software-supported reactive/backup capabilities. Park critical/deferrable demand fractions unless separately reapproved.
6. The 5–15-house **main design space**, the two EPM-vs-proposed-LV electrical studies, 50 first-stage economic anchors, and principal PF 0.95/0.98/1.00 were already discussed and agreed. **Still open**: exact proposed nested 15-site membership and supply-point geometry, topology and conductor choice, and matching source trajectories for remote AC results. No new physical network created in this turn.
7. **Earlier exception to MR discipline**: commit `3e9f526` directly changed `batch_archived_campaign.py` and added its regression assertions without the usual MR. It retained the agreed PF choices but has **not been shown to pass local tests**. Treat as pending independent review, not CI-verified. Do not silently overwrite or roll back.

## 1. Project purpose and boundary

**Overriding study-design decision:** The entire 5-to-15-household sweep, including both 8-house synthetic cases, is the **primary design space regardless of where households ultimately connect**. The existing EPM GIS network and proposed new LV feeder are **two parallel electrical comparison scenarios** using archived equipment designs. The proposed-feeder topology is a gate only for AC validation of that specific new feeder; it must not block archived frontier analysis, fixed-hardware dispatch preparation, or continued existing-EPM AC checks. The main study is not restricted to current EPM transformer territories.


Research a shared, grid-connected community PV+BESS energy system for El Arenal, San Rafael, Antioquia, Colombia. Compare investment cost, 8,760-hour energy self-sufficiency and physical grid capability, accounting for transformer kVA, LV voltage, conductor ampacity, losses and battery SOC. Keep two distinct network questions:

1. **Existing EPM GIS network:** Mapped transformer/node/conductor data with inferred customer service attachments and phase assumptions. These are provisional but physically informed.
2. **Proposed new community LV network:** A new defined 5–15-household group and supply point. It does NOT automatically share existing EPM transformer territories. Transformer 5 kVA is a provisional hypothesis only.

The original September hackathon supplied community framing, HOMER comparison, initial PyPSA/PV+BESS and outage analysis, spatial groupings, governance and household billing. These remain historical background. 35 mapped candidate properties are available, but they are NOT 35 verified service-to-transformer assignments.

The primary investment engine is PyPSA/Linopy, with a perfect-foresight annual energy chronology. Independent pandapower AC checks test network physics. Energy-only optimality or PCC kVA screening does not imply AC feeder feasibility. Perfect foresight is a planning benchmark, NOT MPC or realistic online forecasts.

## 3. Latest engineering decisions

- Include every 5–15 house count and preserve the two 8-house synthetic variants, not an arbitrary six-design subset.
- Principal load PF scenarios: **0.95, 0.98 and 1.00**. PF 0.90 is an *optional* inductive stress assumption, not required of all final equipment.
- Distinguish existing EPM GIS connections from a **proposed new** community network. Do not relocate or duplicate mapped houses by accounting alone.
- Proposed new transformer **5 kVA** is an initial planning sensitivity. Only revisit based on demonstrated hourly voltage/thermal need and documented location/nameplate choices; do not presume it serves all 15 homes.
- Principal proposed engineering voltage band **0.95–1.05 pu**. Preserve **0.97–1.03 pu** as old strict research sensitivity, not official regulation. All AC results remain conditional balanced-feeder proxies.
- Enforce Deye manufacturer **20% physical min SOC** for new studies, hour-to-hour chronology, feasible BESS charge/discharge and inverter AC kVA. Treat nighttime Q support as **off** until manufacturer proof.
- Use original 70/80/90/95% self-sufficiency targets, minimum cost and maximum self-sufficiency for Stage A (50 equipment anchors); remaining nondominated points Stage B, exact transition-boundary designs Stage C (296) and complete archive Stage D. AC candidate expansion should be guided by actual pass/fail and hardware transitions.
- Keep full original 6,840-task investment results, the 35-property spatial evidence, existing source paths, historical figures and tests intact. New network topologies must be labelled hypothetical until real design inputs exist.
- Use manufacturer battery/current, inverter-string and PV constraints before procurement recommendation. Original economic model omits full site cost, physical feeder losses, actual EPM tariff, verified service reconnection cost and some hardware cost increments.

## 4. Unfinished work, organized by dependency (parallel tracks)

**Execution order clarified 6 Nov:** Advance (A) proposed feeder topology specification, (B) 5-to-15-house equipment candidate preparation and manufacturer-SOC annual dispatch, and (C) reuse/reconciliation of existing EPM GIS annual AC evidence **in parallel**. New-feeder AC must wait for A, but neither B nor existing-EPM work must wait for A. The selection does not impose an arbitrary cap: 50 first economic anchors, then sampled nondominated and hardware-transition candidates when AC results justify. Do not claim all 50 require immediate runs. No new historical investment sweep.


### Parallel track A: Define a defensible proposed LV topology, NOT YET SIMULATED

Study protocol exists: studies/household_frontier_reuse_v1/PROPOSED_LV_NETWORK_PROTOCOL.md.

- Choose a consistent proposed transformer/PCC position and **15 nested participating household positions**, keeping a fixed first 5, first 6, … first 15 arrangement.
- Use mapped geographic evidence, clearly distinguishing hypothetical supply routes from actual EPM GIS-owned conductors. Derive conductor type, R/X, ampacity, LV nominal phase/neutral assumptions, service lengths and a defensible radial or branched scheme.
- Define whether one supplied solar inverter at the shared PCC or a separately documented arrangement applies. Make zero-export logic, source voltage, protection and 5 kVA sensitivity explicit.
- First run **load-only baseline** and representative worst-hour voltage, transformer and branch-current checks; document basic plausibility before launching selected PV+BESS AC.

### Parallel track B: Reuse archived investment choices without rerunning MILP

- Read stage A 50 anchors and Stage B/C from studies/household_frontier_reuse_v1/outputs/archived_hardware_pool_5kva.csv; retain **all** household counts.
- Reconstruct full 8,760-hour fixed-hardware operation with manufacturer SOC and realistic power constraints; preserve original source hash and initial objective.
- Verify Deye SE-G5.1 current, 20% SOC, battery DC power sizing, inverter/PV string constraints and zero/incomplete cost proxies.

### Network-specific AC: resume existing GIS checks now; proposed network after track A

- Reuse prior annual 94022/106039 AC and partial campaign results. Run **independent** full-year AC for the proposed new LV network and each relevant selected PF, voltage and route assumption.
- Store convergence, Vmin/Vmax, line and transformer loading, losses, ENS, self-sufficiency and lifecycle-cost impacts; distinguish fixed-energy schedules from true AC-constrained redispatch.
- When AC fails, resample feasible P/Q regions for the same network/PF/topology, reoptimize **all 8,760 chronological SOC hours**, then independently AC-revalidate. Never transfer existing seven-house P constraints to another network.
- Only after validated AC runs form an AC-feasible cost/self-sufficiency Pareto. Targeted finalist PF/route stress must run all 8,760 hours before an annual claim. Do not turn a 77/96 critical-hour sample into annual certification.

### Later gate: Field validity and extensions

- Verify customer meter and actual transformer associations, nameplates (especially 106039), conductor impedances/current ratings, service routes, actual LV phase/split-phase and neutral, household PF, inverter night Q support, manufacturer battery power, actual tariffs, battery replacement and local civil/permit costs.
- Include AC losses and site/interconnection expenses in economics, and test uncertainty. Then move toward resilience/outage, practical forecasting/causal control, and household hosting/reassignment as independent, costed options.

## 7 November 2026: Battery expansion and curtailment interpretation correction

**User explicitly reaffirmed:** Main WP1 strategy is **PV supplies community load, excess PV charges shared DC-coupled battery, stored PV supplies load when PV is low, EPM only makes up unmet demand**. No export AND no grid-to-battery charging in baseline; user considers grid charging economically unattractive relative to consuming otherwise curtailed PV. Keep this as the approved baseline. User asks why we can't add storage to absorb annual PV curtailment, what "larger battery does not automatically eliminate mismatch" means, and why an economic optimizer might not pick larger batteries.

**Critical clarification to previous assistant's oversimplification:** Larger battery *is allowed and deserves a controlled sensitivity*. The 54/9/20 and 56/10/20 designs were **different candidate annualized-cost/self-sufficiency points**, not physical storage maxima; increasing BESS capacity can reduce grid purchases if suitable PV surplus occurs before later shortfalls, but cannot always resolve power bottlenecks, forecast timing, low-solar multiday periods, battery/inverter port limits and economic tradeoffs. An entire year's 6.0/6.3 MWh curtailed PV is NOT a storable reservoir; additional usable battery energy must absorb relevant hours and discharge at later shortfall hours within battery power and SOC limits. Original PyPSA objective `model.py` defaults economic (`annualized capex + 0.29 USD/kWh assumed grid imports`), not maximization of self-sufficiency at unlimited spending; `--mode energy` instead minimizes imports within a fixed capex budget. Original 6,840 task archive included self-sufficiency targets. The quotation and tariff are proxies, not confirmed EPM prices.

**Important new evidence found through direct reanalysis of user-provided original `SEEDExchange_two_12house_annual_AC.zip` 8760h dispatch CSVs:**
- 54 panels, 9 modules: PV DC available 28,524.17 kWh, curtailed 5,993.32 (21.01%), grid imported 1,959.13, exported 0; **1,193 hours of curtailment**, of which **908 curtailment hours while the PyPSA Store was at manufacturer floor** (about **4,759.88 curtailed kWh**).
- 56 panels, 10 modules: PV DC available 29,580.62, curtailed 6,292.02 (21.27%), grid imported 1,273.19, exported 0; **1,182 curtailment hours**, of which **900 curtailment hours while Store at manufacturer floor** (about **5,012.16 curtailed kWh**).
- This is **NOT** the straightforward "battery already full so curtailment proves storage shortage" narrative. The annual PyPSA fixed-hardware dispatch is cost-minimizing and has PERFECT FOREKNOWLEDGE of the full year's PV and demand. The objective doesn't penalize curtailment directly, other than opportunities lost to reduce subsequent grid imports, and battery charging/discharging links have tiny marginal costs. When later same-day PV is sufficient, multiple charge schedules can deliver equivalent annual grid imports. The optimizer may curtail PV in the morning while battery at floor, then charge in the afternoon, because it knows sufficient energy remains. **Example 2025-07-30, 56/10:** PV available at 12:00 = 21.5490 kW, actual PV used 2.2453 kW, curtailed 19.3037 kW, battery usable Store at floor 2.29 kWh, battery charging 0; after 13:00 charges ~10.88 kW in one hour and continues later, ultimately serving evening load. It is demonstrably wrong to explain this hour by a full battery or merely inadequate storage nameplate. This behaviour is a *perfect-foresight/secondary dispatch-objective caveat*, NOT by itself a model bug or energy-shortage causal proof. User wants practical PV charging logic in real operation, so test a **causal PV-first battery-charge scheduling policy** or explicitly justified secondary objective/tie-breaking and verify that grid import, physical losses, lifespan and resilience actually improve or stay controlled. Avoid naive "minimize curtailment" without accounting for battery throughput and cycling wear.
- The **fixed equipment 56/10 vs 54/9** original SOC-corrected annual equipment charge difference is approx **+USD209.68/y**, while annual grid energy difference of **-685.94 kWh × USD0.29/kWh = -USD198.92/y**, net 56/10 annual cost about **+USD10.755/y**. **Not** a clean battery-only comparison because PV panels (54 vs 56), battery count (9 vs 10), and battery DC power limits (15.651 vs 21.12 kW) all change. If studying BESS size, hold panels, inverter and allowable battery power fixed when physically possible, then vary modules alone; separately vary battery charging/discharging power, reserve/causal dispatch and compare PV availability vs curtailment and grid imports.
- Model battery bank: 5.12 kWh nominal/module, 4.325 kWh original model usable Store/module. Physical 20% SOC floor converts to minimum Store energy 0.229 kWh/module; 10-module model Store max 43.25 kWh and allowed span 40.96 kWh. For 9 modules max 38.925 kWh and span 36.864 kWh. Device electrical limitations, SOC and pack current require respect; can't simply use 5.12kWh as 100% usable.
- Existing code in `case1/studies/pypsa_joint_optimization_v1/model.py` only creates `GRID_EXPORT` for `reference_credit`; the PV DC bus serves inverter and battery DC charging links, no grid-to-battery link. The 20%-physical-SOC fixed-hardware module `fixed_hardware.py` checks `grid_export_kw == 0`. Reuse this source, not redesign simulator.

**Proposed next research analyses (do not silently change existing historical objective or model):**
1. **Battery-only energy capacity sensitivity** on the 12-home 56-panel/20kVA configuration, varying e.g. 10→12→14→16 battery modules, same 2025 demand, PV, PF, site and inverter, keeping DC power fixed first to isolate kWh effect, subject to manufacturer module/current/inverter limits. Also include 8/9 modules if hardware feasible, label differences. Original economic archive may already have comparable fixed-panel rows, so first extract/reuse them rather than blindly rerun.
2. Distinct **battery bank DC power (kW)** sensitivity with energy held fixed to isolate charging/discharging bottleneck; compare curtail, grid import, energy lost, number of failed evening hours, net annualized cost, battery throughput/replacement and annual SOC.
3. **Dispatch/causality sensitivity:** perfect-foresight lowest-cost historical reference, PV-first causal charge-when-surplus operation, and optional forecast-limited rolling horizon/survival SOC reserve if resilience tests justify. Cannot infer that early charge always improves cost or reduces imports; compare genuinely.
4. Explore storage sizing vs **resilience** (islanded blackout service after proper GFM and safe switch/protection) as a later WP2-4 research extension; not assume bigger grid-connected battery alone means islanding is supported.
5. Keep strict no export/no grid charging approved baseline. Do not invent EPM export tariff, new capacity limits, or assured continuous device parallel ratings.

- **Nuance:** fixed-BESS series holds module count but optimizer is free to change PV panels, inverter kVA, battery DC power within bounds; other points in full curve DO change. Thus this does **not** isolate module count for a fixed 56-panel/20-kVA reference, nor use manufacturer corrected SOC, AC losses, or pandapower constraints. Do not claim a new 'battery-vs-SS plot' is novel; reuse source figures and tabulated values and, when needed, controlled fixed-hardware slices for the new operational question only.

**Economic/dispatch clarification from source:**
- `model.py` has `GRID_IMPORT` marginal cost **0.29 USD/kWh** (historical cost proxy, NOT verified actual EPM tariff); charge and discharge `Link` each have tiny `marginal_cost = 0.00001` (source's tie-breaking/placeholder), eta_ch=eta_dis=sqrt(.95), annualized battery hardware costs and time-based replacements but no explicit validated per-cycle degradation/wear penalty. Curtailment is represented by non-dispatching `PV_DC` and is not separately penalized.
- Existing objective **already financially penalizes imported energy** far more than per-link marginal throughput, and thus has incentive to store PV *when doing so avoids later imports*. A morning PV surplus curtailed at low battery SOC may occur because the model knows afternoon PV remains abundant and can charge later without additional imports. It is a perfect-foresight indifference / multi-solution effect, not proof battery must be bigger, and not proof existing model prefers grid import to chargeable PV at $0.29.
- Physical efficiency losses mean unnecessary charging/discharging and high sustained SOC can add wear and losses. Blanket punitive curtailment penalty large enough to alter original sizing may overbuild storage/PV or cause gratuitous cycling. **Recommendation for research, not yet code change:** first hold hardware and 8760h profiles fixed; stage 1 solve original **minimum cost including import and legitimate throughput wear**; stage 2 among near-equal cost solutions apply a small/lexicographic PV-utilization preference that prioritizes charging during otherwise-curtailed PV when SOC headroom exists while forbidding excess cycling (battery throughput bound/tie-breaker), and compare a causal PV-first charging controller that cannot see future PV. Keep new dispatch labels distinct from historical perfect-foresight series; preserve energy/PV conservation, 20% manufacturer SOC, bank current, P limits, no import-to-charge, zero export. Quantify actual reduction in grid imports/curtailment, battery throughput/ageing and changes to resilience readiness, and AC recheck where injections change.
- Once this operational question resolved, **optional targeted** fixed-PV/20kVA/constant bank kW battery module sensitivity can isolate the capacity effect, using archived points whenever matched before calculating new fixed hardware cases. This must not sidetrack A nine-house annual equipment feasibility and 7/9/12 existing-GIS PF/line-length robustness.
